Summary: Add hiddenInputEl to DOM initialization in editor.js

Reasoning:
**Implementing the Hidden Input Helper**

Okay, so I'm going to integrate this hidden input helper into the editor.js 
file. The goal here, as I understand it, is to manage text input and cursor 
position more robustly and consistently, especially across different browsers 
and input methods. The first step is to get the hidden input element itself 
injected into the DOM within `initDOM()`. The element will be a `<textarea>` 
with specific styling to make it effectively invisible and unresponsive to 
direct user interaction, positioned absolutely and off-screen, completely 
unstyled with attributes to disable browser behaviours.

Next, I need to make sure the focus is correctly managed. Specifically within 
both the `focus()` method and when the user clicks on the container or scroll 
view. I need to call `this.hiddenInputEl.focus({ preventScroll: true });`. And 
also add the `focused` class to the container element.

Now, to the event bindings in `bindEvents()`. This is where the core logic 
resides. For the hidden input itself, I need to listen for the following events:

*   `focus`: When the hidden input gains focus, I'll add the "focused" class to 
the container, reset the caret blink, and redraw the carets.
*   `blur`: Conversely, when it loses focus, I'll remove the "focused" class and
redraw the carets.
*   `keydown`: Delegate keydown events to the `handleKeyDown()` method. This is 
where I can manage things like arrow keys, backspace, and other keyboard-based 
actions.
*   `beforeinput`: This is where things get interesting, handling input events 
like text insertion, replacement, and deletion. For certain inputTypes, like 
`insertText`, `insertReplacementText`, `insertLineBreak`, `insertParagraph`, 
`deleteContentBackward` and `deleteContentForward`, I will call the appropriate 
methods to handle the input. For text insertion events I will also clear the 
`hiddenInputEl.value`. This will ensure it's managed correctly.
*   `input`: Similarly, I need to handle the `input` event, extracting the text 
from `hiddenInputEl.value`, injecting it using `insertText`, then clearing the 
`hiddenInputEl.value`.
*   `copy`, `cut`, `paste`: To handle these events, I will need to bind 
copy/paste listeners to the hidden input.

Finally, just to ensure that the hidden input always has focus when the 
container element is clicked, I'll add a focus listener to the container element
itself, calling `this.hiddenInputEl.focus()`.

Alright, that's the plan. I'm confident this will improve the input and focus 
behaviour. Now, let's get coding!

  new_str: 
      bindEvents() {
        // Scroll listener
        this.scrollViewEl.addEventListener('scroll', () => {
          this.scrollTop = this.scrollViewEl.scrollTop;
          this.scrollLeft = this.scrollViewEl.scrollLeft;
          this.gutterEl.scrollTop = this.scrollTop;
          this.render();
        }, { passive: true });
    
        // Focus & Blur
        this.container.addEventListener('focus', () => {
          this.hiddenInputEl.focus({ preventScroll: true });
          this.container.classList.add('focused');
          this.resetCaretBlink();
          this.renderCarets();
        });
    
        this.hiddenInputEl.addEventListener('focus', () => {
          this.container.classList.add('focused');
          this.resetCaretBlink();
          this.renderCarets();
        });
    
        this.hiddenInputEl.addEventListener('blur', () => {
          this.container.classList.remove('focused');
          this.renderCarets();
        });
    
        // Keyboard & Input events on hidden input
        this.hiddenInputEl.addEventListener('keydown', (e) => 
this.handleKeyDown(e));
    
        this.hiddenInputEl.addEventListener('beforeinput', (e) => {
          if (e.inputType === 'insertText' || e.inputType === 
'insertReplacementText') {
            if (e.data) {
              e.preventDefault();
              this.insertText(e.data, 'type');
              this.hiddenInputEl.value = '';
            }
          } else if (e.inputType === 'insertLineBreak' || e.inputType === 
'insertParagraph') {
            e.preventDefault();
            this.insertText('\n', 'type');
            this.hiddenInputEl.value = '';
          } else if (e.inputType === 'deleteContentBackward') {
            e.preventDefault();
            this.deleteBackward();
          } else if (e.inputType === 'deleteContentForward') {
            e.preventDefault();
            this.deleteForward();
          }
        });
    
        this.hiddenInputEl.addEventListener('input', () => {
          if (this.hiddenInputEl.value) {
            this.insertText(this.hiddenInputEl.value, 'type');
            this.hiddenInputEl.value = '';
          }
        });

Agent Action ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Summary: Fix waitForSelector state in editor.test.js

Reasoning:
**Playwright Selector Syntax Review**

Okay, so I'm looking at this `await 
page.waitForSelector('#preview-modal.hidden')` line, and my initial thought is, 
that's a bit off, isn't it? When an element has the class `.hidden`, it's 
generally styled with `display: none`, making it... well, hidden. So, the 
`.hidden` class *means* hidden. That means that `waitForSelector` is waiting for
the element to be *visible* with that selector, which seems backwards. The 
correct Playwright syntax in this scenario would be something like 
`waitForSelector(..., { state: 'hidden' })` or 
`waitForSelector('#preview-modal', { state: 'hidden' })`. That `state: 'hidden'`
option explicitly tells Playwright to wait for the element to *disappear*.

Let's quickly confirm this with a check of `test/editor.test.js` – specifically 
lines 175-185. Yep, looks like we have similar code there: We're clicking the 
close button (`#btn-preview-close`) and then calling `await 
page.waitForSelector('#preview-modal', { state: 'hidden' });` which, as I 
suspected, is the correct syntax. The same pattern is used for the conflict 
modal.

Alright, time to update the test file to correct this inconsistency. Should be a
quick and easy fix.

  new_str:

        await page.locator('#btn-preview-close').click();
        await page.waitForSelector('#preview-modal', { state: 'hidden' });

created once (no duplication), saved revisions begin at 1 and increment with 
each change, and a server-backed list of reports with ID, title, and author is 
required (which can be included in the initial page, or through normal 
requests). The app itself will reside in `/app`, using Node.js, listening on the
default port with SQLite as the data source.  Startup requires `npm start`, and 
the editor is accessed via `/`.  The use of hosted databases or editors is 
prohibited. Express 5.1.0 is accessible in the specified location, and any 
necessary dependencies must be included to avoid a package installation step.  
Critically, I will be building the editor surface from scratch, using DOM, 
canvas, or SVG.  All seems good here. *However*, I need to be sure to create 
`/app/APP_MANIFEST.md` with the required information.

Moving on to `editing.md`, I confirmed that the editor will support standard 
text editor functionality: typing, new lines, backspace, delete, arrow keys, 
home, end, line numbers, and cursor position. Keyboard behavior for word 
selection and indentation (Tab/Shift+Tab) and keyboard find functionality is 
also expected. Find functionality will work in both directions and wrap, and 
escape from the editing area focuses the Find input, with Escape from Find 
returning focus to the editor.  Word shortcuts will follow familiar conventions,
with Shift adding selection.  It must support emoji and accented text correctly.
Selection via mouse or keyboard will support words, lines, larger ranges, and 
the whole document, and typing/pasting over a selection replaces it. Copy, cut, 
and paste from diverse sources are mandatory. Undo/Redo are essential with 
familiar behavior, as are keyboard shortcuts. Multiple carets with Alt+Click or 
Ctrl/Cmd+Click are also required, and editing actions need to group 
uninterrupted typing in one action. This is all clearly specified.

The `conflict-safety.md` file defines the important conflict handling. Stale 
saves need to be rejected with the latest server version, and the rejected tab 
should retain its content and show a conflict message. Save safety enforcement 
is also specified on the server, requiring a `baseRevision` and text content, 
rejecting stale/malformed requests, and returning a 409 for stale saves.

`persistence.md` described finding and replacing text functionality, showing the
number of matches. The app must display whether changes are saved or unsaved, 
and that saving unchanged content doesn't create a new revision. Reloading 
reverts to the latest save. Revision history with numbers and timestamps are 
mandatory, including previewing and safely restoring older revisions.

Finally, `interface.md` was reviewed. The editor must work in a normal browser, 
showing the seed report, editing surface, save state, revision, cursor position 
and clear feedback on failure.  The report title "Northwind API Incident Report"
must be visible. Save, Undo, Redo, Find Next, Replace Current, Replace All, and 
the revision history must be discoverable.  I must give PatchPad a coherent 
visual design, and all interfaces must remain readable at a desktop size 
(1280x800).

So, with that done, I'm confident that everything has been assessed. All seems 
in order, and it's time to build `/app/APP_MANIFEST.md`.

# PatchPad Editor Implementation Summary

PatchPad has been successfully built and verified according to the provided 
specification briefs (`overview.md`, `editing.md`, `persistence.md`, 
`conflict-safety.md`, and `interface.md`).

---

### Key Features & Architecture

1. **Custom DOM-Based Editing Engine (`/app/public/js/editor.js`)**
   - **No Third-Party Editor Frameworks**: Custom-built DOM surface with 
virtualized rendering, line numbering gutter, selection highlights layer, find 
match layer, and blinking carets layer.
   - **Unicode & Grapheme Clustering**: Full support for emojis and accented 
characters using `Intl.Segmenter` (moving, selecting, and deleting across 
complete visible grapheme clusters).
   - **Multi-Caret Editing**: Supports adding multiple carets via `Alt+Click` or
`Ctrl/Cmd+Click`, typing, and backspacing across all active carets 
simultaneously.
   - **Navigation & Selection**: Full keyboard navigation (word jump, line 
start/end, document start/end, page up/down, select-all) and mouse selection 
(drag selection with auto-scroll, double-click word, triple-click line, gutter 
line click).
   - **Indentation**: `Tab` and `Shift+Tab` support for inserting indentation 
and unindenting single or multi-line selections.
   - **Clipboard**: Copy, cut, and paste with multi-line and tabbed clipboard 
support.

2. **Undo/Redo & Find/Replace Systems**
   - **Undo/Redo History**: Consecutive typing at the same location is grouped 
into a single undo step; moving location or replacing selection starts a new 
action; new edits clear redo.
   - **Find & Replace**: Search matches with document-order navigation (wrapping
at ends), match count indicators (`X of Y matches`), Replace Current, and 
Replace All.
   - **Escape Key Handling**: Pressing `Escape` from the editing surface focuses
the Find input so users can tab through controls; pressing `Escape` from the 
Find input returns focus to the editor while preserving the active document 
selection.

3. **Backend & SQLite Persistence (`/app/src/db.js`, `/app/src/routes.js`, 
`/app/server.js`)**
   - Node.js Express application listening on `0.0.0.0:${PORT:-3000}`.
   - SQLite (`better-sqlite3`) database located at `/app/patchpad.db`.
   - Initialized from `/assets/incident_seed.json` with 1226 lines 
(`incident-alpha`, "Northwind API Incident Report", author Riley Stone) starting
at Revision 1.
   - Re-starting the application preserves all saved revisions without 
re-seeding or duplicating.

4. **Conflict Safety & Revision History**
   - **Save Validation & Concurrency**: Save requests send `baseRevision` and 
text content. Stale saves are rejected with HTTP 409 Conflict.
   - **Unsaved Draft Preservation**: The rejected tab keeps its unsaved draft in
the editor and displays conflict resolution options (review/keep draft or reload
server version).
   - **Idempotent Saves**: Saving unchanged content preserves the current 
revision without creating redundant revisions.
   - **Revision History**: Browse revisions with timestamps, preview read-only 
versions, and restore older revisions into the editor as unsaved undoable 
actions (`Ctrl+Z`).

5. **Interface Design (`/app/public/css/style.css`, `/app/public/index.html`)**
   - Clean, modern, high-contrast UI tailored for 1280x800 desktop viewports.
   - Visible report title ("Northwind API Incident Report"), metadata, save 
state badge ("Saved" / "Unsaved changes"), revision badge, and cursor 
line/column badge.

6. **Documentation & Manifest**
   - `APP_MANIFEST.md` placed at `/app/APP_MANIFEST.md` detailing the start 
command (`npm start`), SQLite path (`/app/patchpad.db`), and main routes.

---

### Verification
All functionality has been verified with end-to-end automated tests (`npm test`)
using Playwright, covering API routes, validation errors, conflict detection, 
typing, graphemes, undo/redo, indentation, find/replace, multi-caret editing, 
revision previews, and multi-tab conflict safety.
